Remaining acceptance blocker: [current reference/capture audit](../artifacts/fidelity-review/2026-10-07/remaining-acceptance-blockers/ASSESSMENT.md) rechecks all nine open gates and finds their historical/current comparisons still unavailable. Windows Chrome now reports 177 processes, 60.29 GiB summed working set and 67.30 GiB private; attribution remains unknown and browser launches stay stopped. Reference research found no new usable original recording or working target runtime. Installed source-derived visuals/lifecycle/loadout and the completed free-rotation fix do not prove the missing comparisons; the near-floor defect remains open. No additional evidence-supported fidelity patch was established. The full goal is blocked pending reference/capture access, not completed or reduced; acceptance remains 31/40.

Latest production physics correction: [separate free angular sampling from collision attempts](../artifacts/fidelity-review/2026-10-07/angular-sampling-budget/REPORT.md) repairs lost movement time in fast collision-free rotation in both joint and standalone solvers. Selected candidates, including rejected refreshes, retain the 48-attempt ceiling; total sweep work is separately bounded at 512 per substep, with explicit joint exhaustion reasons. The authentic near-floor failure and incomplete-frame caller handling remain unresolved. No browser/server started; checklist stays 31/40 and all nine gates remain open.

Independent [weapon review](../artifacts/fidelity-review/2026-10-06/soldier-weapon-review/WEAPON_REVIEW_PLAN.md), [soldier reference plan](../artifacts/fidelity-review/2026-10-06/soldier-weapon-review/V26_REFERENCE_PLAN.md) and [scope/hurt/results audit](../artifacts/fidelity-review/2026-10-06/soldier-weapon-review/V22_SOURCE_AUDIT.md) identify the remaining direct comparisons. Those source audits alone established no new completed requirement; acceptance at that stage was30/40.

Updated 2026-10-06 for the request to align the copy with **every point** in the forty-item [checklist](FIDELITY_CHECKLIST.md). All forty points have a disposition below. This is an implementation pass, not proof of exact original reproduction.

The user reconfirmed **Woodlands / Barrack** as the intended look. Their recorded terrain, buildings, menu, HUD and weapon presentation guide this work.

The default Barrack and Woodlands maps now render the recovered publisher scene meshes, original texture images and stored model transforms. Client and server use the same concave triangle geometry for movement, support and combat queries. Six recovered spawn positions per map replace the authored positions; the original sphere radius 25 and eye offset 85 are normalized to the copy's 1.72 eye height. This normalization and grounded joining are explicit adaptations, not recovered physical units.

**The full goal remains active.** Original scenery replaces the earlier blockouts in solo and online modes. All six original first-person weapons and the thirteen Barrack automatic doors are now installed, including animation-aware collision, authoritative opening and recovered door audio. Recovered GI soldiers now replace authored soldiers in default-map bots and remote players, with source skin, motion, held guns and death sinking. Source scoring and scavenging rules are installed. Native animation timing, remaining combat/physics equivalence, combat audio mixing, effect registration and historically registered comparison cameras remain unverified. The dated pass notes below preserve earlier implementation stages; the current rows supersede their blockout limitations.

Source weapon controls now use shared per-callback counter state for all six weapons, including initial readiness and waits across selection changes. Held numeric selection follows firing and remains available during reload. Chat preserves the source edition distinction: Barrack freezes its counter, while Woodland continues decrementing. Historical callback timing and network clock phase remain explicit adapters.

## Historical follow-up corrections from the saved references

These notes preserve the earlier authored implementation stages. The current checklist rows and later recovered-source integrations supersede their source geometry, scoring, scope and artwork limitations.

After permissions changed, [sharper Woodland evidence](research-evidence/2026-09-30/REFERENCE_OBSERVATIONS.md) supported another correction pass: bright red/yellow lowercase weapon rows, vivid chat, a Woodland-only outlined cross/score layout, continuous dark earth, mottled stone buildings, fine branch-shaped foliage, and a broader angular blue-gray/amber-wood legacy AK. The score uses the copy's scoring formula; no original formula is claimed.

- Barrack now includes four sparse isolated trees in playable grass, with shared collidable trunks and clearance from routes, buildings and spawn/pickup locations. Barrack 2010 sheet 0 row 1 column 5 and row 2 column 1 support this distribution; positions are authored rather than recovered.
- Sloped Barrack access ramps now have solid triangular concrete side/end profiles, shared combat raycasts and client/server movement checks. Level roof bridges stay open underneath. Barrack 2012 sheet 1 rows 2–3 supports this broad shape; exact original dimensions remain unresolved.

- Woodlands pitched-roof gables now face outward. Exterior/interior raycast tests verify the front-face winding. The larger authored farmhouse now has a flat roof, retaining its position and dimensions; Woodland sheet 0 row 3 columns 4–5 supports roof-family diversity rather than exact topology.
- GAME OVER now shares the full-screen menu artwork. The scope aperture is approximately 80% of the shorter viewport dimension, following the broad size visible in Barrack sheet samples; exact reticle geometry is still unmeasured.
- Legacy aiming is limited to the Dragunov following the [surviving player guide](https://www.markwiering.nl/games.htm); the Modern preset retains all-weapon aiming.

- A further source review found a white crosshair and prominent clouds in the captured Woodland copy, while the sharp original shows red outer strokes, rounded black gameplay corners and a restrained gray-blue sky. Woodland now has red strokes without a center dot, rounded canvas corners and subdued map-specific sky uniforms. Fresh low-tier HUD and map-camera captures were inspected; updated Woodland high-tier soldier/terrain rendering also passes.
- The timber shed now has shallow wall rails, uprights and diagonal braces, following the [11-second original frame](research-evidence/2026-09-30/woodland-video-11s.jpg). The `shed` camera captures these surfaces. Scene tests prove the beams remain against the walls and leave opposed doorways clear; the original shed's exact dimensions remain unknown. Refreshed rendered review led to removal of unsupported timber side panes and lighter weathered planks.
- Woodland weapon rows now scale to roughly 15% of desktop viewport height, and chat uses broader bold text following the sharp sources. Desktop, wide-source-aspect and compact renders were inspected; compact rows remain clear of health and chat stays clear of the emblem. Legacy soldiers now use exposed skin on both hands at both quality tiers, retaining their hand geometry and joints; Modern gloves remain unchanged.
- Arrow controls are enabled by default, retaining saved opt-outs. Recovered Legacy controls use Left/Right steering, Up/Down forward/back and A/D strafe, with Left priority. Modern retains lateral arrow strafing. Actual client and both-map browser checks verify steering without lateral movement, page-scroll prevention and chat isolation. Nominal steering is150°/s from5° per source30FPS frame; original wall-clock timing remains unmeasured.

## Meaning of status

- **Implemented:** the source change is present; checks are described in the evidence column. Visual changes still require browser review unless stated otherwise.
- **Partial:** a useful part is implemented, but the point's full acceptance evidence is missing.
- **Evidence pending:** investigated, with an explicit retained behavior or research limit. This is not a completed correction.

## Historical broad validation — 2026-10-01

The [RPG convex integration validation](../artifacts/fidelity-review/2026-10-01/current/source-rpg-convex-validation.json) records **1,170 client/shared +336 server =1,506 unit tests**, both TypeScript checks and the production build. All **twelve unique affected browser cases pass across recorded runs**; the initial Woodland visibility failures and corrected real-input route remain preserved. These checks do not prove the thirteen open fidelity requirements.

Recovered normal-frame UI and ammunition text are installed and checked in both editions at three viewport sizes. The latest full suites pass **1,180 client/shared+336 server=1,516 tests** and the production build; all **thirteen unique affected browser cases pass across recorded runs**. Source hints/input layout and native font/raster/projection equivalence remain open.

### Historical validation before the recovered-source integrations

- Browser and server TypeScript checks and the production Vite build pass.
- Latest building/fence pass: **33 focused client/shared tests across four files** and **16 server feature tests** pass. Actual client movement verifies open-bay/gate traversal and closed-wall/fence collision. These counts overlap the full suite.
- Client/shared Vitest suite: **564 tests pass across 69 files**, including actual movement and chat code, scene/resource disposal, weapons and transport validation.
- Full server suite: **162 tests pass across eleven files**, including 17 real HTTP/WebSocket transport tests.
- First live browser pass: 14 of 15 fidelity/game cases passed. The HUD slot check was interrupted by a bot kill; it now uses an empty real room and passes in the follow-up run. The complete nine-case fidelity suite then passed, including a Woodland HUD/rifle capture with no browser console or page errors. After rendering refinements, all five affected chat/capture/room-discovery cases passed again. Final low-tier screenshots were inspected and copied into the comparison board. The last emblem-size refinement also passed its HUD/rifle browser capture with no console/page errors.
- Native browser capture/keyboard fallback checks and Control+W protection pass in the game suite.
- All five multiplayer browser cases pass, covering real join/movement/disconnect, blast/self-damage, rejected fire reconciliation and capture races. Both updated Woodland soldier browser cases pass at low/high quality with no console/page errors; actual screenshots were inspected. The final combined five-case pass also verifies the HUD/rifle and all twelve map-camera captures after building/fence changes.
- The broad 37-case suite was intentionally interrupted during its expensive archived-winter high-tier effects case to prioritize updated-map captures. Its low-tier effects case passed; the full broad suite has not passed.

At that historical validation stage, the development preview on port8859 and room server on port8081 returned healthy HTTP responses. This records the completed check rather than claiming those processes are still running.

## Original-map activation review

Both default maps now use original mesh parts, texture bindings and evaluated stored transforms. The four solo/online low-quality source-scene cases pass, as do seven source control/reload/health and AK/scope regressions (eleven affected browser cases total). All fourteen source scenery review stations were refreshed and inspected selectively, including the Woodland barn, cottage, shed corner and Barrack roof/ramp approach. The first subsequent camera/high-quality run exposed a Woodland server correction; the later source movement/path follow-up resolves it, and all six final source-map cases pass.

V05, V06, V08 and V11 are now accepted at their landscape/composition scope: original tree placement/cards, original banks/fences, and original building shapes/placements replace the authored alternatives. This brings acceptance to **27/40**. Source shader precision, edition identity, camera registration and interior contrast remain explicit limits; V07, V10, V12 and V14 remain open. Barrack ramp support includes a source-derived climb/descent regression; V08 is accepted for recovered connected roof/ramp composition and verified actual client/server ramp traversal; dynamic doors and complete route tracing remain V10.

The original Barrack [door audit](research-evidence/2026-09-30/ORIGINAL_DOOR_ANIMATION.md) preserves all thirteen tracks and 282 keys. A standalone runtime sampler and packaged exact recovered key values are prepared, but default gameplay does not yet use them. Door collision, shot visibility, authoritative opening and audio still require integration. Original GI and weapon runtime replacements remain separate incomplete work.


The source ramp/online follow-up fixes spherical support at the upper ramp edge and a microscopic contact gap that previously accumulated falling velocity. The original-map server contact tolerance now matches the client. Bounded movement paths preserve actual120Hz sphere steps across curved terrain and obstacles, instead of checking only the chord between render-frame endpoints. Every sample retains sphere collision, support, flight and travel-budget checks; malformed/oversized paths, hidden peaks, wall crossings and excessive travel reject. Protocol4 remains additive; frames are bounded to64KiB and128 samples per transform. Transport queues and batches every sample, clears stale history on authoritative reset/reconnect/boost, and retries unsent samples.

Refill still uses elapsed server time; Winter retains the previous3-unit cap. Actual Game/server tests cover full Barrack ramp ascent/descent, fifteen seconds of Woodland movement, one-second catch-up, authorized RG6 paths and rejection boundaries. Full root suites pass **564 client/shared +162 server =726 tests**; TypeScript and production builds pass. Original numerical Havok/combat parity remains open.


Final live checks pass for all **six** original-map solo/online/quality cases, both real online RG6 ground-shot/landing cases, and real multiplayer movement/disconnect and shared blast/self-damage. All six source-scene JSON artifacts record `verified:true` and no server corrections. Their low/high captures were inspected. The fourteen review-camera captures previously passed and selected originals were inspected. Production TypeScript/Vite builds pass; final root suites remain726 passing tests.

The separate solo RG6 check passes in Barrack but fails in Woodland at the recovered first spawn: the shot spends ammo and damages the player, but the observed rise is about1.09 rather than the authored boost test's required2 units. This remains an explicit incomplete copy-mechanics regression, not a permission problem or original-map movement failure. The old test also used authored ground heights and coordinates; landing checks now inspect actual grounded/nonpenetrating state, and setup uses the recovered spawn. Original grenade physics has a timed bouncing fuse and no explosion impulse, so G06 remains open for source behavior rather than claiming this authored boost as fidelity.

## Source hitscan and held-reload follow-up

Recovered scripts replace the default-map player/authority hitscan values: pistol12, SPAS36 aggregated from two identical18-damage rays, AK6 and Dragunov36. Both modes use central camera rays with no random pellet spread or headshot multiplier. Source camera recoil applies after the resolved ray: pitch1/7/1/3degrees and discrete yaw−.5/0/+.5degrees. Holding fire repeats for every source weapon rather than imposing authored semi-automatic input. Four hitscan repeating intervals use Woodland8/15/2/50 callbacks and Barrack9/16/3/51 callbacks, divided by nominal30Hz. This elapsed-time adapter does not reproduce first-shot or cross-weapon counter state; explosives still use authored numerical rules.

Held R now restarts the reload wait at sampled nominal30Hz before firing, including full chambers. Empty reload selects the first usable source slot without transfer or a new lock; unusable numeric slots are rejected. A player with all stores empty becomes unarmed, retains that flag across reconnect, cannot authorize shots, and selects a collected weapon before reloading. The equipped enum retains only a cached identity when unarmed. [Source controls](research-evidence/2026-09-30/ORIGINAL_WEAPON_CONTROLS.md) document original unrestricted switching during waits, repeated numeric selection and precise input/counter-order differences that still need correction.

Current validation: **651 client/shared tests across82files +201 server tests across14files =852 tests**, client/server TypeScript and production build pass; modified browser specs also type-check. **Eight affected browser cases pass**: both source maps hold R through real authority with no firing, then fire the held pistol repeatedly after release; all four solo/online map loadout cases verify the Dragunov refund; and both AK presentation/authoritative-refill checks pass. New actual-Game tests resolve every source hitscan weapon/head target and held-input isolation; server tests cover damage/cadence/duplicate-hit rejection and unarmed fallback/reconnect/pickup restoration. Optional unarmed loadouts propagate through real NetworkClient callbacks; malformed values and inconsistent reload state are rejected. Acceptance remains **27/40**; numerical/script progress does not close the thirteen broader unproved fidelity requirements.


## Shared weapon counter and input-order follow-up

The previous elapsed cadence adapter is replaced by `shared/originalWeaponCounter.ts`. Client and authoritative rooms preserve the original signed `waitgun`/`poldwaitgun` state through selection, reload and reconnect. Barrack evaluates the saved difference before incrementing and applies the source cross-weapon reset rule; Woodland decrements before its readiness gate. Successful held-R reload runs inside that callback. A standalone pickup reload remains outside it, preserving Barrack's additional callback before release. The RPG assignment description in this historical stage was incomplete: Barrack resets the shot counter before reload, while Woodland resets after construction. [The later correction](research-evidence/2026-10-01/ORIGINAL_RPG_COUNTER_INTEGRATION.md) preserves these intermediate stages and recovered reload audio.

Actual Game callbacks fire the current gun before selecting the lowest held numeric slot, repeat usable-slot equip presentation, allow switching during a wait, and run before later pickup/bot updates. Empty RPG reload is polled at readiness even without fire input. Chat suppresses gameplay input while preserving each edition's counter behavior, including pickups received while typing. Offline pause shifts both callback clocks. Source transform changes flush before following selection/reload requests so the authority receives equipment and typing state in order. Optional validated counter snapshots preserve compatibility with older loadout envelopes.

Current validation: **718 client/shared tests across84files +212 server tests across15files =930 tests** pass. Production browser/server TypeScript and build pass; the four affected browser specifications also type-check. After the final callback ordering change, **four real-server Chromium cases pass**: held R followed by held pistol fire, switching during reload, lowest held numeric priority, and typing counter behavior on each map. They assert no page or server errors and save the linked control JSONs. Earlier eight loadout/presentation/control browser checks also passed before that final ordering change. Actual-Game counter tests cover initial/cross-weapon waits, firing-before-selection, standalone pickup and remembered typing, render stalls and pause.

The implementation schedules nominal30Hz callbacks, bounds render-stall replay to one second and anchors received counter snapshots to local receipt time. These adapters do not prove native Director timing or exact network phase. Source explosive/Havok movement, skinned actor hit triangles, audio/effects and historical camera registration remain incomplete. Acceptance remains **27/40**; the thirteen open checklist requirements remain active.

The next physics correction is grounded in [recovered explosive rules](research-evidence/2026-09-30/ORIGINAL_EXPLOSIVE_RULES.md): RG6 detonation on callback59 after bouncing, RPG velocity/proximity/collision handling, sixteen callbacks of close-range blast damage and the source.05-second Havok step. The current impact blast and detonation boost remain authored; this audit does not claim their replacement.

## October1 source health and death integration

Default-map local health now carries canonical H and the recovered ±2 flick phase in shared client/server arithmetic. Each completed source goody callback flips the phase before projectile actors apply damage. Damage bypasses armor and spawn immunity, retains signed living health, and kills only when H + 2c < 0. A phase flip alone never retests death. Snapshots, damage events and reconnect carry explicit phase and its completed callback epoch; bounded client prediction restores authority without applying damage twice. Explicitly supplied health pickups add twenty uncapped and five score; no fixed health/armor placements were invented. Winter retains its authored armor, protection and health rules.

Current validation passes **837 client/shared tests across93files +258 server tests across18files =1,095 tests**, TypeScript and production build. **Ten browser cases pass**: four source fuse/blast cases, four held jump/landing cases, and two actual online health/death/replay cases. [Barrack health](../artifacts/fidelity-review/2026-10-01/current/barrack-source-health.json) and [Woodland health](../artifacts/fidelity-review/2026-10-01/current/woodlands-source-health.json) each record a surviving first blast at20 health, a second blast ending under the literal death expression (current Barrack trace H0/c−2, Woodland H−5/c−2), no automatic revival, and fresh Replay health100, with no page/server errors. Shared and runtime-equipped client/server unit checks cover both strict boundary phases, uncapped health and reconnect; the live traces cover the observed lethal phase, not every boundary combination. [Validation manifest and logs](../artifacts/fidelity-review/2026-10-01/current/source-health-validation.json) preserve this pass separately from earlier runs. The build retains the existing Vite chunk-size warning.

Acceptance remains **27/40** with all thirteen open requirements active. Native hurt rotation/audio, out-of-range Director HUD clipping, historical message/callback ordering, skinned hit triangles and native contact response remain unproved. The four earlier assisted-jump failures remain unresolved; passing ordinary jumping does not accept them.

Lethal damaged-first and killed-first messages preserve the attacker before disconnect; self blast uses the actual callsign. Snapshot-only unknown attribution stays explicit. Replay returns to entry. Historical score submission and frame54 artwork remain unresolved.

[The movement foundation](research-evidence/2026-10-01/ORIGINAL_REMOTE_MOVEMENT_INTEGRATION.md) was initially staged; [source transport integration](research-evidence/2026-10-01/ORIGINAL_REMOTE_VIEW_TRANSPORT.md) now enables the recurrence in the actual Barrack/Woodland Game. The earlier foundation-stage limitation below is retained for context. A review found that 20Hz snapshot resets produce271–304ms of presentation lag before network delay and independently combine lagged position/yaw with the latest skeletal phase. Authority currently queries300ms of raw pose history, which cannot reconstruct that displayed combination. Enabling the recurrence requires a per-viewer mirror of acknowledged server-issued snapshots and the renderer callback timeline. Increasing hit volumes or raw-history duration is insufficient. The new shared mirror is integration groundwork; production remotes retain their prior interpolation pending full wire/client/server validation.

Acceptance remains **27/40**. V22/V26/G05/G06 remain open alongside the other nine open requirements.

[Final endgame/mirror validation](../artifacts/fidelity-review/2026-10-01/current/source-endgame-validation.json) passes **1,049 client/shared tests across105 files +293 server tests across19 files =1,342 tests**, TypeScript/build and **four actual death/Replay browser cases** (both maps, solo/online). All four result captures were inspected. An aligned91callback renderer/mirror test compares742 skinned vertices per pose within1e−9 and21 GI ray distances; that numerical foundation does not prove transport integration.


## Source remote transport integration — 2026-10-01

The actual source Game now uses one global renderer callback clock and an ordered snapshot/fire/death receipt stream. Authority freezes viewer-specific GI poses reconstructed from actual sent packets; negotiated source shots cannot fall back to raw pose history. Resume resets the generation while retaining identity, wounded health and ammunition. Dead disconnected actors remain visible until the recovered76-callback fade completes, including catch-up. [Transport evidence and limits](research-evidence/2026-10-01/ORIGINAL_REMOTE_VIEW_TRANSPORT.md) distinguish implementation from pending final live verification.

The current1,403-unit-test run passes. Both moving-target live cases now pass after monotonic observation, removal of repeated pure collision queries and immediate invalid-generation suspension. They verify actual accepted shots, reconstructed displayed GI poses and preserved wounded identity/ammunition on intentional resume. That initial eleven-of-twelve failure is resolved by the source timing correction below; final checks pass all twelve cases. No receipt budget or hit volume was widened. V26/G06 and the other eleven open points remain open; acceptance is27/40.

### 2026-10-06 — reachable Solo pickup order corrected

Default Solo previously captured drops in insertion order but collected them backwards. An unarmed player touching two drops could equip the newer weapon first, changing selection and both reload outcomes. Game now processes the supplied capture in order, skips retired entries and excludes uncaptured newborns; archival traversal remains unchanged. This preserves the declared callback adapter and recovered per-pickup selection/reload dependencies, without asserting native actor-list scheduling. [Independent audit and executed regressions](../artifacts/fidelity-review/2026-10-06/gameplay-followup/REPORT.md) preserve that distinction.
